   498	/-- **Theorem 1, splitting (full paper statement)**: for `n ≥ 2`, the
   499	hypothesis `(A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1` alone suffices — no witnessing pair
   500	`p ≠ q` is needed.  When `|A ∩ B| ≥ 2` we extract a pair and invoke
   501	`IsTaut.splits`; when `|A ∩ B| ≤ 1` we enlarge both sides by the same
   502	fresh vertices `F` to reach `|A' ∩ B'| = 2 ≤ n + 1`, run the split along
   503	`· ⊆ A'`, then transfer it back to `· ⊆ A`.  The transfer is valid because
   504	every generator of the filling `M` lies in `A ∪ B`, hence is disjoint from
   505	the fresh set `F`, so `t ⊆ A' ↔ t ⊆ A` on `M.support`.
   506	
   507	`[Infinite V]` only guarantees that fresh cut vertices are available for the
   508	`|A ∩ B| ≤ 1` enlargement; a taut filling never uses vertices beyond its boundary
   509	(`IsTaut.vert_subset`). -/
   510	theorem IsTaut.splits_full {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V] [Infinite V]
   511	    {A B : Finset V} {n : ℕ} (hn : 2 ≤ n) (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1)
   512	    {X Y : Chain V}
   513	    (hX : ∀ s ∈ X.support, s ⊆ A ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   514	    (hY : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   515	    (hXc : bdry X = 0) (hYc : bdry Y = 0)
   516	    {M : Chain V} (hMt : IsTaut M) (hM1 : bdry M = X + Y) :
   517	    bdry (M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)) = X ∧
   518	    bdry (M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A)) = Y ∧
   519	    IsTaut (M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)) ∧
   520	    IsTaut (M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A)) ∧
   521	    M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A) + M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = M := by
   522	  classical
   523	  by_cases h2 : 2 ≤ (A ∩ B).card
   524	  · obtain ⟨p, hp, q, hq, hpq⟩ := Finset.one_lt_card.mp h2
   525	    exact IsTaut.splits hn hp hq hpq hC hX hY hXc hYc hMt hM1
   526	  · push_neg at h2
   527	    have hn1 : 1 ≤ n := by omega
   528	    have hle : (A ∪ B).card ≤ (A ∪ B).card + (2 - (A ∩ B).card) := Nat.le_add_right _ _
   529	    obtain ⟨W, hWsub, hWcard⟩ :=
   530	      Infinite.exists_superset_card_eq (A ∪ B) _ hle
   531	    set F := W \ (A ∪ B) with hF
   532	    have hFdisj : Disjoint F (A ∪ B) := Finset.sdiff_disjoint
   533	    have hFcard : F.card = 2 - (A ∩ B).card := by
   534	      have hkey : F.card + (A ∪ B).card = (A ∪ B).card + (2 - (A ∩ B).card) := by
   535	        rw [hF, Finset.card_sdiff_add_card_eq_card hWsub, hWcard]
   536	      omega
   537	    set A' := A ∪ F with hA'
   538	    set B' := B ∪ F with hB'
   539	    have hinter : A' ∩ B' = (A ∩ B) ∪ F := by
   540	      ext x
   541	      simp only [hA', hB', Finset.mem_inter, Finset.mem_union]
   542	      constructor
   543	      · rintro ⟨hxA | hxF, hxB | hxF⟩
   544	        · exact Or.inl ⟨hxA, hxB⟩
   545	        · exact Or.inr hxF
   546	        · exact Or.inr hxF
   547	        · exact Or.inr hxF
   548	      · rintro (⟨hxA, hxB⟩ | hxF)
   549	        · exact ⟨Or.inl hxA, Or.inl hxB⟩
   550	        · exact ⟨Or.inr hxF, Or.inr hxF⟩
   551	    have hABFdisj : Disjoint (A ∩ B) F :=
   552	      (hFdisj.mono_right (Finset.inter_subset_left.trans Finset.subset_union_left)).symm
   553	    have hcard' : (A' ∩ B').card = 2 := by
   554	      rw [hinter, Finset.card_union_of_disjoint hABFdisj, hFcard]
   555	      omega
   556	    have h1lt : 1 < (A' ∩ B').card := by rw [hcard']; omega
   557	    obtain ⟨p, hp, q, hq, hpq⟩ := Finset.one_lt_card.mp h1lt
   558	    have hC' : (A' ∩ B').card ≤ n + 1 := by rw [hcard']; omega
   559	    have hAA' : A ⊆ A' := Finset.subset_union_left
   560	    have hBB' : B ⊆ B' := Finset.subset_union_left
   561	    have hX' : ∀ s ∈ X.support, s ⊆ A' ∧ s.card = n + 1 :=
   562	      fun s hs => ⟨(hX s hs).1.trans hAA', (hX s hs).2⟩
   563	    have hY' : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B' ∧ s.card = n + 1 :=
   564	      fun s hs => ⟨(hY s hs).1.trans hBB', (hY s hs).2⟩
   565	    obtain ⟨hbx', hby', hMxt', hMyt', hsum'⟩ :=
   566	      IsTaut.splits hn hp hq hpq hC' hX' hY' hXc hYc hMt hM1
   567	    have hbdsupp : ∀ s ∈ (bdry M).support, s.card = n + 1 := by
   568	      intro s hs
   569	      rw [hM1] at hs
   570	      rcases Finset.mem_union.mp (Finsupp.support_add hs) with h | h
   571	      · exact (hX s h).2
   572	      · exact (hY s h).2
   573	    have hpure : ∀ s ∈ M.support, s.card = n + 2 :=
   574	      fun s hs => hMt.dim_pure hbdsupp s hs
   575	    have hdim2 : ∀ s ∈ M.support, 2 ≤ s.card := by

   384	    (hsupp : ∀ s ∈ M.support, s ⊆ W ∧ s.card = k) (hk : W.card < k) :
   385	    M = 0 := by
   386	  rw [← Finsupp.support_eq_empty]
   387	  by_contra h
   388	  obtain ⟨s, hs⟩ := Finset.nonempty_iff_ne_empty.mpr h
   389	  obtain ⟨h1, h2⟩ := hsupp s hs
   390	  have := Finset.card_le_card h1
   391	  omega
   392	
   393	/-- `Z_{k-1}(W)` is trivial when `|W| = k ≥ 1`. -/
   394	lemma eq_zero_of_closed_supp_card_eq {W : Finset V} {k : ℕ} (hk : 1 ≤ k)
   395	    {M : Chain V} (hsupp : ∀ s ∈ M.support, s ⊆ W ∧ s.card = k)
   396	    (hW : W.card = k) (hM : bdry M = 0) : M = 0 := by
   397	  have hsub : M.support ⊆ {W} := by
   398	    intro s hs
   399	    obtain ⟨h1, h2⟩ := hsupp s hs
   400	    rw [Finset.mem_singleton]
   401	    exact Finset.eq_of_subset_of_card_le h1 (by omega)
   402	  have hMeq : M = Finsupp.single W (M W) :=
   403	    Finsupp.support_subset_singleton.mp hsub
   404	  obtain ⟨x, hx⟩ := Finset.card_pos.mp (show 0 < W.card by omega)
   405	  have hbW : bdryGen W (W.erase x) = sgn x W := by
   406	    rw [bdryGen, Finset.sum_apply']
   407	    rw [Finset.sum_eq_single_of_mem x hx]
   408	    · rw [Finsupp.smul_apply, Finsupp.single_apply, if_pos rfl, smul_eq_mul,
   409	        mul_one]
   410	    · intro y hy hyx

 succeeded in 0ms:
   230	of that dimension are trivial. -/
   231	theorem Kmap_eq_zero_of_closed {A B : Finset V} {p : V} {n : ℕ} (hn : 1 ≤ n)
   232	    (hp : p ∈ A ∩ B) (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1) {Y : Chain V}
   233	    (hY : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1) (hYc : bdry Y = 0) :
   234	    Kmap A p Y = 0 := by
   235	  have hsupp : ∀ t ∈ (Kmap A p Y).support, t ⊆ A ∩ B ∧ t.card = n + 1 := by
   236	    intro t ht
   237	    rw [Kmap_eq_sum] at ht
   238	    obtain ⟨s, hs, hts⟩ := Finset.mem_biUnion.mp (Finsupp.support_finset_sum ht)
   239	    have hts' : t ∈ (KGen A p s).support :=
   240	      Finsupp.support_smul hts
   241	    obtain ⟨hsub, hcard⟩ := KGen_support hts'
   242	    obtain ⟨hsB, hsn⟩ := hY s hs
   243	    constructor
   244	    · intro x hx
   245	      rcases Finset.mem_union.mp (hsub hx) with h | h
   246	      · obtain ⟨hxs, hxA⟩ := Finset.mem_inter.mp h
   247	        exact Finset.mem_inter.mpr ⟨hxA, hsB hxs⟩
   248	      · rw [Finset.mem_singleton] at h
   249	        exact h ▸ hp
   250	    · rw [hcard, hsn]
   251	  rcases Nat.lt_or_ge (A ∩ B).card (n + 1) with hlt | hge
   252	  · exact eq_zero_of_supp_card_lt hsupp hlt
   253	  · have hcard : (A ∩ B).card = n + 1 := le_antisymm hC hge
   254	    have hNc : bdry (Kmap A p Y) = 0 := by
   255	      rw [bdry_Kmap A (Finset.mem_inter.mp hp).1, hYc, map_zero]
   256	    exact eq_zero_of_closed_supp_card_eq (by omega) hsupp hcard hNc

   258	/-! ## The kill lemma (the types analysis, in general dimension) -/
   259	
   260	/-- Every `(n+2)`-simplex with vertices in `A ∪ B` is killed by `K_{A,p}`
   261	or by `K_{B,q}`, provided `p ≠ q` lie in `A ∩ B` and
   262	`(A ∩ B).card ≤ n+1`. -/
   263	theorem Kkills_or_Kkills {A B : Finset V} {p q : V} {n : ℕ}
   264	    (hp : p ∈ A ∩ B) (hq : q ∈ A ∩ B) (hpq : p ≠ q)
   265	    (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1) {t : Finset V}
   266	    (htAB : t ⊆ A ∪ B) (htcard : t.card = n + 2) :
   267	    Kkills A p t ∨ Kkills B q t := by
   268	  by_contra h
   269	  obtain ⟨hA, hB⟩ := not_or.mp h
   270	  rw [Kkills, not_and_or, not_not, not_not] at hA hB
   271	  -- the three-part count: t = (t ∩ (A∩B)) ⊎ (t \ A) ⊎ (t \ B)
   272	  have hdisj : Disjoint (t \ A) (t \ B) := by
   273	    rw [Finset.disjoint_left]
   274	    intro x hxA hxB
   275	    rcases Finset.mem_union.mp (htAB (Finset.mem_sdiff.mp hxA).1) with h | h
   276	    · exact (Finset.mem_sdiff.mp hxA).2 h
   277	    · exact (Finset.mem_sdiff.mp hxB).2 h
   278	  have hsplit : t \ (A ∩ B) = (t \ A) ∪ (t \ B) := by
   279	    ext x
   280	    simp only [Finset.mem_sdiff, Finset.mem_inter, Finset.mem_union]
   281	    tauto
   282	  have hpart : (t ∩ (A ∩ B)).card + ((t \ A).card + (t \ B).card) = n + 2 := by
   283	    have h1 := Finset.card_inter_add_card_sdiff t (A ∩ B)
   284	    rw [hsplit, Finset.card_union_of_disjoint hdisj] at h1
   285	    omega
   286	  have hinter : t ∩ (A ∩ B) ⊆ A ∩ B := Finset.inter_subset_right
   287	  have hintercard := Finset.card_le_card hinter
   288	  rcases hA with hA | ⟨hxa, hpt⟩ <;> rcases hB with hB | ⟨hxb, hqt⟩
   289	  · have : t ⊆ A ∩ B := Finset.subset_inter hA hB
   290	    have := Finset.card_le_card this
   291	    omega
   292	  · have hxa0 : (t \ A).card = 0 :=
   293	      Finset.card_eq_zero.mpr (Finset.sdiff_eq_empty_iff_subset.mpr hA)
   294	    have hc : (t ∩ (A ∩ B)).card = n + 1 := by omega
   295	    have heq : t ∩ (A ∩ B) = A ∩ B :=
   296	      Finset.eq_of_subset_of_card_le hinter (by omega)
   297	    exact hqt (Finset.mem_inter.mp (heq ▸ hq)).1
   298	  · have hxb0 : (t \ B).card = 0 :=
   299	      Finset.card_eq_zero.mpr (Finset.sdiff_eq_empty_iff_subset.mpr hB)
   300	    have hc : (t ∩ (A ∩ B)).card = n + 1 := by omega
   301	    have heq : t ∩ (A ∩ B) = A ∩ B :=
   302	      Finset.eq_of_subset_of_card_le hinter (by omega)
   303	    exact hpt (Finset.mem_inter.mp (heq ▸ hp)).1
   304	  · have hc : (t ∩ (A ∩ B)).card = n := by omega
   305	    have hqn : q ∉ t ∩ (A ∩ B) := fun h => hqt (Finset.mem_inter.mp h).1
   306	    have hpn : p ∉ insert q (t ∩ (A ∩ B)) := by
   307	      intro h
   308	      rcases Finset.mem_insert.mp h with h | h
   309	      · exact hpq h
   310	      · exact hpt (Finset.mem_inter.mp h).1
   311	    have hsub : insert p (insert q (t ∩ (A ∩ B))) ⊆ A ∩ B := by
   312	      intro x hx
   313	      rcases Finset.mem_insert.mp hx with rfl | hx
   314	      · exact hp
   315	      · rcases Finset.mem_insert.mp hx with rfl | hx
   316	        · exact hq
   317	        · exact hinter hx
   318	    have := Finset.card_le_card hsub
   319	    rw [Finset.card_insert_of_notMem hpn,
   320	      Finset.card_insert_of_notMem hqn] at this
   321	    omega

   323	/-! ## Theorem 1, part 1: additivity of Zvol -/
   324	
   325	lemma exists_fill_eq_Zvol {X : Chain V} (W : Chain V) (hW : bdry W = X) :
   326	    ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = Zvol X := by
   327	  obtain ⟨M, h1, h2⟩ :=
   328	    Nat.sInf_mem (⟨nrm W, W, hW, rfl⟩ :
   329	      {m | ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = m}.Nonempty)
   330	  exact ⟨M, h1, h2⟩
   331	
   332	/-- The mass of `M` is at most the mass killed by `K_{A,p}` plus the mass
   333	killed by `K_{B,q}`, when every generator is killed on at least one side. -/
   334	lemma nrm_le_killed_add_killed {A B : Finset V} {p q : V} {M : Chain V}
   335	    (hkill : ∀ t ∈ M.support, Kkills A p t ∨ Kkills B q t) :
   336	    nrm M ≤ nrm (M.filter (Kkills A p)) + nrm (M.filter (Kkills B q)) := by
   337	  classical
   338	  have hsubA : (M.filter (Kkills A p)).support ⊆ M.support := by
   339	    rw [Finsupp.support_filter]; exact Finset.filter_subset _ _
   340	  have hsubB : (M.filter (Kkills B q)).support ⊆ M.support := by
   341	    rw [Finsupp.support_filter]; exact Finset.filter_subset _ _
   342	  rw [nrm_eq_sum_subset hsubA, nrm_eq_sum_subset hsubB,
   343	    ← Finset.sum_add_distrib, nrm]
   344	  refine Finset.sum_le_sum fun s hs => ?_
   345	  rw [Finsupp.filter_apply, Finsupp.filter_apply]
   346	  rcases hkill s hs with h | h
   347	  · rw [if_pos h]
   348	    omega
   349	  · rw [if_pos h]
   350	    split <;> omega
   351	
   352	/-- **Theorem 1, additivity** (Ellison): for an almost disjoint union
   353	`X + Y` — `X` a closed pure `n`-chain on `A`, `Y` on `B`, with
   354	`(A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1` witnessed by two distinct shared vertices
   355	`p ≠ q ∈ A ∩ B` — the filling volume adds:
   356	`Zvol (X + Y) = Zvol X + Zvol Y`. -/
   357	theorem Zvol_add_of_almost_disjoint {A B : Finset V} {p q : V} {n : ℕ}
   358	    (hn : 1 ≤ n) (hp : p ∈ A ∩ B) (hq : q ∈ A ∩ B) (hpq : p ≠ q)
   359	    (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1)
   360	    {X Y : Chain V}
   361	    (hX : ∀ s ∈ X.support, s ⊆ A ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   362	    (hY : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   363	    (hXc : bdry X = 0) (hYc : bdry Y = 0) :
   364	    Zvol (X + Y) = Zvol X + Zvol Y := by
   365	  have hpA : p ∈ A := (Finset.mem_inter.mp hp).1
   366	  have hqB : q ∈ B := (Finset.mem_inter.mp hq).2
   367	  have hXYc : bdry (X + Y) = 0 := by rw [map_add, hXc, hYc, add_zero]
   368	  obtain ⟨Mx, hMx, hMxn⟩ := exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p X) (bdry_cone_of_closed hXc)
   369	  obtain ⟨My, hMy, hMyn⟩ := exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p Y) (bdry_cone_of_closed hYc)
   370	  obtain ⟨M, hM1, hM2⟩ :=
   371	    exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p (X + Y)) (bdry_cone_of_closed hXYc)
   372	  refine le_antisymm ?_ ?_
   373	  · have hbd : bdry (Mx + My) = X + Y := by rw [map_add, hMx, hMy]
   374	    calc Zvol (X + Y) ≤ nrm (Mx + My) := Zvol_le hbd
   375	      _ ≤ nrm Mx + nrm My := nrm_add_le _ _
   376	      _ = Zvol X + Zvol Y := by rw [hMxn, hMyn]
   377	  · have hMt : IsTaut M := by rw [IsTaut, hM1]; exact hM2
   378	    have hbdsupp : ∀ s ∈ (bdry M).support, s.card = n + 1 := by
   379	      intro s hs
   380	      rw [hM1] at hs
   381	      rcases Finset.mem_union.mp (Finsupp.support_add hs) with h | h
   382	      · exact (hX s h).2
   383	      · exact (hY s h).2
   384	    have hpure : ∀ s ∈ M.support, s.card = n + 2 :=
   385	      fun s hs => hMt.dim_pure hbdsupp s hs
   386	    have hdim2 : ∀ s ∈ M.support, 2 ≤ s.card := by
   387	      intro s hs
   388	      rw [hpure s hs]
   389	      omega
   390	    have hvert : vert M ⊆ A ∪ B := by
   391	      refine (hMt.vert_subset hdim2).trans ?_
   392	      rw [hM1]
   393	      refine (vert_add_subset X Y).trans ?_
   394	      exact Finset.union_subset_union
   395	        (vert_subset_of_supp fun s hs => (hX s hs).1)
   396	        (vert_subset_of_supp fun s hs => (hY s hs).1)
   397	    have hsuppAB : ∀ t ∈ M.support, t ⊆ A ∪ B :=
   398	      fun t ht => (supp_subset_vert ht).trans hvert
   399	    have hbx : bdry (Kmap A p M) = X := by
   400	      rw [bdry_Kmap A hpA, hM1, map_add,
   401	        Kmap_eq_self (fun s hs => (hX s hs).1),
   402	        Kmap_eq_zero_of_closed hn hp hC hY hYc, add_zero]
   403	    have hby : bdry (Kmap B q M) = Y := by
   404	      rw [bdry_Kmap B hqB, hM1, map_add,
   405	        Kmap_eq_self (fun s hs => (hY s hs).1),
   406	        Kmap_eq_zero_of_closed hn (Finset.inter_comm A B ▸ hq)
   407	          (Finset.inter_comm A B ▸ hC) hX hXc, zero_add]
   408	    have h1 := nrm_Kmap_add_killed_le A p M
   409	    have h2 := nrm_Kmap_add_killed_le B q M
   410	    have h3 : nrm M ≤ nrm (M.filter (Kkills A p)) + nrm (M.filter (Kkills B q)) :=
   411	      nrm_le_killed_add_killed fun t ht =>
   412	        Kkills_or_Kkills hp hq hpq hC (hsuppAB t ht) (hpure t ht)
   413	    have h4 : Zvol X ≤ nrm (Kmap A p M) := Zvol_le hbx
   414	    have h5 : Zvol Y ≤ nrm (Kmap B q M) := Zvol_le hby
   415	    omega
   416	
   417	/-- **Theorem 1, additivity (full paper statement)**: the hypothesis
   418	`(A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1` alone (with `n ≥ 1`) suffices — no witnessing pair
   419	`p ≠ q` is needed.  When `|A ∩ B| ≥ 2` we extract a pair and invoke
   420	`Zvol_add_of_almost_disjoint`; when `|A ∩ B| ≤ 1` we enlarge both `A` and
   421	`B` by the same fresh vertices to reach exactly `|A' ∩ B'| = 2` (still
   422	`≤ n + 1`), which never changes `X`, `Y`, or the conclusion.
   423	
   424	`[Infinite V]` is pure ambient bookkeeping, not a finiteness restriction: chains are
   425	finitely supported (`Chain V := Finset V →₀ ℤ`), so the active vertex set is always finite.
   426	The hypothesis only guarantees that *fresh cut vertices are available* for the `|A ∩ B| ≤ 1`
   427	enlargement above (the paper's "add a brand-new point to `C` if necessary"); a taut filling
   428	never uses vertices beyond its boundary (`IsTaut.vert_subset`), so the unused ambient vertices
   429	play no mathematical role. -/
   430	theorem Zvol_add_of_almost_disjoint_full {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V] [Infinite V]

    26	\title{Splitting and the Stickerball Theorem for Taut Fillings\\[2pt]
    27	\large A proof extracted from the Lean formalization}
    28	\author{}
    29	\date{}
    30	
    31	\begin{document}
    32	\maketitle
    33	
    34	\noindent
    35	This document records, as ordinary mathematics, the two theorems established in the Lean
    36	development \code{lean/Taut}: (II) taut fillings split under almost-disjointness, and
    37	(III) a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere is an \emph{AnyRootedStickerball}.
    38	The organizing spine is the Lean architecture, not any prior narrative: almost-disjoint
    39	splitting is the engine, and the stickerball theorem is a single induction on the size of
    40	the filling driven by that engine. Typewriter names are the Lean identifiers; their status
    41	is summarized in \S\ref{sec:status}. Everything stated as a theorem or lemma is fully
    42	formalized with no \code{sorry} and no project axioms (\S\ref{sec:status}); the one notion
    43	that is genuinely weaker than its classical namesake, and the one classical bridge that is
    44	\emph{not} proved, are isolated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.
    45	
    46	%==========================================================================
    47	\section{Definitions}\label{sec:defs}
    48	
    49	Fix a linearly ordered vertex set $V$. A simplex is a finite subset $s\subseteq V$; if
    50	$\card{s}=k+1$ we call it a $k$-simplex. A \emph{chain} is a finitely supported function
    51	$M$ from simplices to $\Z$ (\code{Chain V}), written $M=\sum_s M(s)\,s$, with orientations
    52	fixed by the vertex order. The boundary $\bd$ is the usual signed face operator
    53	(\code{bdry}); $\bd\bd=0$. The \emph{norm} is $\norm{M}=\sum_s\card{M(s)}$ (\code{nrm}); a
    54	chain is \emph{simplicial} if every coefficient lies in $\{-1,0,1\}$
    55	(\code{SimplicialChain}), and then $\norm{M}$ is the number of simplices in its support.
    56	
    57	\begin{definition}[Filling volume; taut filling]\label{def:taut}
    58	For a cycle $X$ (a chain with $\bd X=0$), the \emph{filling volume} is
    59	\[
    60	\Zvol(X)=\min\{\norm{M}:\bd M=X\}\qquad(\code{Zvol}).
    61	\]
    62	A chain $M$ is a \emph{taut filling} of $X$ if $\bd M=X$ and $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X)$, i.e.\ $M$
    63	is a norm-minimal filling of its own boundary (\code{IsTaut}).
    64	\end{definition}
    65	
    66	Tautness contributes exactly three things to the proofs, all consequences of minimality:
    67	\begin{itemize}
    68	\item[(T1)] \emph{Boundary control:} $\bd M=X$ holds on the nose.
    69	\item[(T2)] \emph{Purity / no junk:} the generators of a taut filling of an $n$-cycle are
    70	all $(n{+}1)$-simplices (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}), and every vertex of $M$ lies on the
    71	boundary (\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}, via \code{IsTaut.no\_internal\_vertex}).
    72	\item[(T3)] \emph{No extraneous cancellation:} a taut chain contains no \emph{nontrivial}
    73	complete cone $\operatorname{cone} x\,W$ as a subchain --- $W$ closed ($\bd W=0$), the apex $x$
    74	absent from $W$, and $W$ nonzero (\code{not\_taut\_complete\_cone}); such a cone could be
    75	refilled with strictly fewer simplices.
    76	\end{itemize}
    77	``Taut'' is therefore purely combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality with these structural
    78	corollaries; it is not a metric or curvature condition.
    79	
    80	\begin{definition}[Combinatorial $2$-sphere]\label{def:sphere}
    81	A set $\sigma$ of triangles is a \emph{combinatorial $2$-sphere} (\code{IsSphere2}) if it is
    82	pure $2$-dimensional, every edge lies in exactly two triangles, every vertex link is a
    83	connected cycle, the $1$-skeleton is connected, and $V+F=E+2$. These properties are taken
    84	as the definition.
    85	\end{definition}
    86	
    87	\begin{definition}[AlmostDisjoint]\label{def:ad}
    88	Fix $n\ge 1$ and $A,B\subseteq V$. Closed pure $n$-cycles $X,Y$ are
    89	\emph{AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$} if every generator of $X$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $A$,
    90	every generator of $Y$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $B$, $\bd X=\bd Y=0$, and
    91	\[
    92	\card{A\cap B}\ \le\ n+1 .
    93	\]
    94	Mathematically: the two regions overlap in at most an $n$-simplex's worth of vertices.
    95	This is the \emph{entire} separation hypothesis --- no metric, no topological transversality.
    96	(Informal name; in Lean it is the hypothesis bundle of
    97	\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} and \code{IsTaut.splits\_full}.)
    98	\end{definition}
    99	
   100	For the stickerball theorem we build complexes of tetrahedra one at a time. Let $\tau$ be a
   101	set of tetrahedra ($4$-element simplices) with current boundary $B$ (a set of triangles).
   102	
   103	\begin{definition}[Clean glue step; clean shelling]\label{def:clean}
   104	Adding a tetrahedron $t$ to $\tau$ is a \emph{clean glue step} (\code{CleanGlueStep}) if:
   105	\begin{itemize}
   106	\item[(a)] $t$ meets the current boundary $B$ in one or two of its four triangular faces,
   107	and the new boundary is $B$ updated by symmetric difference with the faces of $t$;
   108	\item[(b)] $t\notin\tau$ and no already-interior face of $t$ is rogue (each old face of $t$
   109	lies in a shared boundary triangle); and the three \emph{link certificates}:
   110	\item[(c1)] each triangle of $t$ lies in at most one tetrahedron of $\tau$ (so at most two
   111	after adding $t$);
   112	\item[(c2)] each edge of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s edge-link there;
   113	\item[(c3)] each vertex of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s vertex-link there.
   114	\end{itemize}
   115	A \emph{clean shelling} of $\tau$ with boundary $B$ is an ordering $t_1,\dots,t_k$ of $\tau$
   116	in which $t_1$ is a single tetrahedron and each $t_{i+1}$ is added to $\{t_1,\dots,t_i\}$ by
   117	a clean glue step, terminating at boundary $B$ (\code{IsCleanShelling},
   118	\code{CleanShellFrom}).
   119	\end{definition}
   120	
   121	The certificates (c1)--(c3) are precisely the hypotheses needed to preserve, across one
   122	insertion, the three conditions defining a \emph{clean $3$-complex}.
   123	
   124	\begin{definition}[Clean $3$-complex]\label{def:clean3}
   125	A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{clean $3$-complex} (\code{IsClean3Complex}) if it is
   126	pure ($3$-dimensional), triangle-bounded (every triangle in at most two tetrahedra), and
   127	\emph{normal}: both its edge-links and its vertex-links are connected.
   128	\end{definition}
   129	
   130	\begin{definition}[Stickerball; root; RootedStickerball; AnyRootedStickerball]\label{def:sb}
   131	A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{stickerball} with boundary $B$ if it admits a clean
   132	shelling ending at $B$ and $B\neq\varnothing$ (\code{IsStickerball}).
   133	A \emph{root} of a stickerball is a tetrahedron $t\in\tau$ at which some clean shelling can
   134	\emph{begin}. The stickerball is \emph{rooted at $t$} (a \emph{RootedStickerball}) if $t$ is
   135	a root, and is an \emph{AnyRootedStickerball} if every tetrahedron of $\tau$ is a root
   136	(\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball} $=$ \code{IsStickerball} together with
   137	\code{FreelyCleanShellable}, where \code{FreelyCleanShellable} asserts a clean shelling
   138	beginning at each $t\in\tau$).
   139	\end{definition}
   140	
   141	\begin{remark}
   142	``RootedStickerball'' and ``AnyRootedStickerball'' are stated in Lean-backed terms. The
   143	clean shelling certifies normality (\S\ref{sec:classical}); we do not silently upgrade
   144	``stickerball'' to ``shellable ball'' or ``$3$-ball'' --- the proved bridge and the missing
   145	bridge are stated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.
   146	\end{remark}
   147	
   148	%==========================================================================
   149	\section{Splitting under AlmostDisjoint}\label{sec:split}
   150	
   151	The engine of the splitting theorem is a small-support triviality (Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp})
   152	together with the no-cone property (T3) of taut chains. We also record here, once, the one
   153	homological input --- $H_1=0$ and the cut it yields (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}); the splitting theorem
   154	does \emph{not} use it (its analogue of closedness is the coefficient-level
   155	\code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}, not the cut), but the sphere-cutting reductions of \S\ref{sec:main} do.
   156	
   157	\subsection*{The homological input (used in \S\ref{sec:main})}
   158	
   159	\begin{lemma}[$H_1=0$ and the cut]\label{lem:cut}
   160	On a combinatorial $2$-sphere, over $\F_2$, every $1$-cycle is a boundary:
   161	$\operatorname{im}\bd_2=\ker\bd_1$ (\code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}). Consequently, if
   162	$\gamma$ is a triple of vertices whose three edges lie in $\sigma$ but which is not a face
   163	of $\sigma$, then the $1$-cycle of its edges bounds a set $W$ of faces of $\sigma$
   164	(\code{exists\_cut}).
   165	\end{lemma}
   166	
   167	\begin{proof}
   168	The $\F_2$ $2$-cycles are spanned by the all-faces chain (dual-graph connectivity), so
   169	$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_2=F-1$; the connected $1$-skeleton gives
   170	$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_1=V-1$, hence $\dim\ker\bd_1=E-(V-1)$, which equals $F-1$ by
   171	$V+F=E+2$. With $\operatorname{im}\bd_2\subseteq\ker\bd_1$ this forces equality. A non-face
   172	triple $\gamma$ has each vertex on exactly two of its edges, so its edge-chain is a
   173	$1$-cycle and therefore a boundary $\bd_2 W$.
   174	\end{proof}
   175	
   176	\begin{lemma}[Small-support triviality]\label{lem:smallsupp}
   177	A closed chain whose generators all have $n+1$ vertices, supported on a set of at most
   178	$n+1$ vertices, is zero: with fewer than $n+1$ vertices there is no $(n{+}1)$-generator at all
   179	(\code{eq\_zero\_of\_supp\_card\_lt}, closedness not needed), and on exactly $n+1$ vertices the
   180	single possible generator is killed by closedness (\code{eq\_zero\_of\_closed\_supp\_card\_eq}).
   181	\end{lemma}
   182	
   183	\begin{proof}
   184	On at most $n+1$ vertices there is at most one $(n{+}1)$-subset, so the chain is $c\cdot s$
   185	for a single simplex; $\bd(c\,s)=0$ forces $c=0$.
   186	\end{proof}
   187	
   188	\subsection*{The split}
   189	
   190	Fix a common vertex $p\in A\cap B$. There is a norm-non-increasing \emph{kill operator}
   191	$K_{A,p}$ (\code{Projection.lean}, a cone projection toward $p$) with: $K_{A,p}$ fixes every
   192	chain confined to $A$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_self}); and $K_{A,p}$ annihilates every closed pure
   193	$n$-cycle confined to $B$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_zero\_of\_closed}), because its image is a closed
   194	pure cycle on $A\cap B$, hence on $\le n+1$ vertices, hence zero by
   195	Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}. Symmetrically for $K_{B,q}$ with $q\in A\cap B$, $q\neq p$.
   196	
   197	\begin{theorem}[Additivity]\label{thm:add}
   198	If $X,Y$ are AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 1$, then
   199	$\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol(X)+\Zvol(Y)$ (\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full}).
   200	\end{theorem}
   201	
   202	\begin{proof}
   203	$(\le)$ The sum of minimal fillings of $X$ and $Y$ is a filling of $X+Y$, so by subadditivity of
   204	the norm, $\Zvol(X{+}Y)\le\norm{M_X}+\norm{M_Y}=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$ (\code{Zvol\_le}, \code{nrm\_add\_le}).
   205	$(\ge)$ Let $M$ be a minimal (hence taut) filling of $X+Y$, so $\Zvol(X{+}Y)=\norm M$; its
   206	generators are pure $(n{+}2)$-simplices lying in $A\cup B$ (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure},
   207	\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}). Each generator $t$ splits as
   208	$(t\cap A\cap B)\sqcup(t\setminus A)\sqcup(t\setminus B)$; as $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1<n+2$,
   209	$t$ cannot avoid both $t\setminus A$ and $t\setminus B$, so $t$ is killed by \emph{at least
   210	one} of $K_{A,p},K_{B,q}$ (\code{Kkills\_or\_Kkills}, an inclusive ``or''). Equivalently the
   211	two retained sets are disjoint --- a generator may be killed by both (retained by neither),
   212	e.g.\ one with $\card{t\setminus A}=\card{t\setminus B}=2$, and that is harmless. With the
   213	per-map bound $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}\le\norm M$
   214	(\code{nrm\_Kmap\_add\_killed\_le}) and
   215	$\norm M\le\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_B}}$
   216	(\code{nrm\_le\_killed\_add\_killed}) this gives $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\le\norm{M}$.
   217	Because $K_{A,p}$ fixes the $A$-part of $\bd M$ and kills its closed $B$-part, $K_{A,p}M$ fills
   218	$X$ and $K_{B,q}M$ fills $Y$; thus $\norm{M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. (Exclusivity --- each generator
   219	killed by \emph{exactly} one --- is not used here; it holds only later, for the taut filling in
   220	Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, once \code{no\_double\_kill} rules out double kills.)
   221	\end{proof}
   222	
   223	\begin{theorem}[Splitting under AlmostDisjoint]\label{thm:split}
   224	Let $X,Y$ be AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 2$, and let $M$ be a taut filling of
   225	$X+Y$. Then, writing $M_A=M\!\restriction_{\,\subseteq A}$ and
   226	$M_B=M\!\restriction_{\,\not\subseteq A}$,
   227	\[
   228	M=M_A+M_B,\qquad \bd M_A=X,\qquad \bd M_B=Y,
   229	\]
   230	and $M_A,M_B$ are themselves taut (\code{IsTaut.splits\_full}).
   231	\end{theorem}
   232	
   233	\begin{proof}
   234	Tautness enters precisely here, turning Theorem~\ref{thm:add} into rigidity. Since $M$ is
   235	taut, $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$, while
   236	$\norm{M}\ge\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. So all inequalities are
   237	equalities, with two consequences.
   238	
   239	\emph{No double kill} (\code{no\_double\_kill}): if some generator were retained by neither
   240	operator, the middle sum would be strictly smaller than $\norm M$. Hence every generator is
   241	retained by exactly one side, and $K_{A,p}M,K_{B,q}M$ are \emph{taut} fillings of $X,Y$.
   242	
   243	\emph{No straddling simplex} (\code{no\_extreme\_hybrid}). The three-part decomposition
   244	leaves, besides pure generators ($\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$), only ``extreme hybrids'':
   245	a single vertex $y_0$ on the far side and none in $A\cap B$. If such hybrids existed,
   246	collect those through a fixed $y_0$; their faces opposite $y_0$ form a nonempty cycle $L$,
   247	closed by a coefficient-level computation --- $\bd U$ carries no face through $y_0$ because such
   248	faces lie in neither $X$ nor $Y$ (\code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}), \emph{not} via the $\F_2$ cut --- so
   249	$M$ would contain the complete cone $y_0\ast L$, contradicting (T3). This is the only place $n\ge 2$ is used:
   250	the opposite faces need $\ge 2$ vertices for $L$ to be a genuine cycle.
   251	
   252	Thus every generator of $M$ is $\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$, so the literal restriction
   253	gives $M=M_A+M_B$, and $M_A,M_B$ coincide with $K_{A,p}M,K_{B,q}M$; the boundary identities
   254	and the tautness of the pieces follow.
   255	\end{proof}
   256	
   257	\begin{remark}[Why exactly AlmostDisjoint]
   258	The proof uses the separation hypothesis only through Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}, which needs
   259	nothing beyond $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$. There is no appeal to any geometric or topological
   260	transversality; AlmostDisjointness is the exact combinatorial content that makes the cross

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba notes/taut-lean-proof.tex | sed -n '260,476p'" in /Users/doyle/Dropbox/taut
 succeeded in 0ms:
   260	transversality; AlmostDisjointness is the exact combinatorial content that makes the cross
   261	terms vanish.
   262	\end{remark}
   263	
   264	The rational analogue (\code{Qvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full},
   265	\code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}) follows by clearing denominators, applying
   266	Theorems~\ref{thm:add}--\ref{thm:split} over $\Z$, and rescaling.
   267	
   268	%==========================================================================
   269	\section{Taut fillings are AnyRootedStickerballs}\label{sec:main}
   270	
   271	We now prove the main theorem by induction on the number of tetrahedra, using
   272	Theorem~\ref{thm:split} (in its $2$-sphere ``cut'' form) to produce the smaller taut
   273	fillings. First, the engine that gives the filling its $3$-dimensional shape.
   274	
   275	\begin{theorem}[Taut fillings are pure and triangle-bounded]\label{thm:pm}
   276	Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$
   277	(\code{UnitOn X $\sigma$}), and $M$ a taut simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then every
   278	generator of $M$ is a tetrahedron and every triangle lies in at most two of them
   279	(\code{taut\_isPseudomanifold}; purity from \code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}).
   280	\end{theorem}
   281	
   282	\begin{proof}
   283	Purity is (T2): $X$ is $2$-dimensional, so a taut filling is $3$-dimensional.
   284	Triangle-boundedness is a strong induction on the number of tetrahedra, by the trichotomy
   285	used again below. \emph{Base} ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices): $M$ is a single tetrahedron.
   286	\emph{Degree-$3$ vertex}: cut its star (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}), apply the inductive
   287	hypothesis to the smaller filling, re-glue. \emph{No degree-$3$ vertex}: take two disjoint
   288	tetrahedra $e,u$ each meeting the boundary in exactly two faces
   289	(\code{aleph\_disjoint\_eligible\_pair}), remove $e$, apply the inductive hypothesis, re-glue;
   290	the key point is the \emph{triangle rule-out} (\code{faceCount\_removeTet\_sharedFace\_eq\_zero}):
   291	a shared face of $e$ cannot reappear after removing $e$, since the disjoint witness $u$ would
   292	otherwise force that triangle's incidence to $3$ in the inductively triangle-bounded filling
   293	$M-u$.
   294	\end{proof}
   295	
   296	\begin{theorem}[Main]\label{thm:anyrooted}
   297	Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$, and $M$ a taut
   298	simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then the set of tetrahedra of $M$ is an
   299	\emph{AnyRootedStickerball} with boundary $\sigma$
   300	(\code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball}).
   301	\end{theorem}
   302	
   303	By Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} the support consists of tetrahedra; $\sigma\neq\varnothing$ gives
   304	nonempty boundary. The content is that every tetrahedron is a root:
   305	
   306	\begin{theorem}\label{thm:freely}
   307	Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}, for every tetrahedron $t_\star$ of $M$
   308	there is a clean shelling of $M$ beginning with $t_\star$ (\code{theorem3\_clean}, via the
   309	induction skeleton \code{theorem3\_core\_clean} with cases \code{base\_free\_clean},
   310	\code{deg3\_step\_clean}, \code{prime\_step\_clean}).
   311	\end{theorem}
   312	
   313	\begin{proof}
   314	Strong induction on the number $N$ of tetrahedra of $M$. The inductive invariant is exactly
   315	the AnyRooted statement: a clean shelling beginning at \emph{any} prescribed $t_\star$. The
   316	cases are a trichotomy on $\sigma$. In each non-base case the cut (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut})
   317	supplies strictly smaller taut fillings of smaller spheres to which the inductive hypothesis
   318	applies --- by Theorem~\ref{thm:split} itself in the degree-$3$ case, and by a separate
   319	side-decomposition resting on the same cut in the prime case.
   320	
   321	\medskip
   322	\noindent\textbf{Base ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices).} Then $\sigma$ is a tetrahedron
   323	boundary and $M$ is a single tetrahedron $T$; the one-term shelling starts at $T$ and is
   324	vacuously clean.
   325	
   326	\medskip
   327	\noindent\textbf{Degree-$3$ vertex $v$.} The link of $v$ is a triangle $\gamma$, and the
   328	star of $v$ is one tetrahedron $T=v\ast\gamma$. Since $\card{\sigma}\ge 5$, $\gamma$ is a
   329	non-face triangle whose edges lie in $\sigma$, so by Lemma~\ref{lem:cut} replacing the three
   330	triangles at $v$ by $\gamma$ yields a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ with one fewer vertex. Restricting
   331	$M$ to the non-star side is a \emph{direct instance of Theorem~\ref{thm:split}}, with the two
   332	capped vertex-sets as $A,B$ (their overlap is exactly $\gamma$, so $\card{A\cap B}=n{+}1=3$):
   333	the lemma \code{taut\_splits\_for\_capped\_cut} invokes \code{IsTaut.splits} verbatim. It
   334	produces a \emph{taut} filling $M'$ of $\sigma'$ with strictly fewer tetrahedra, so by
   335	induction $M'$ is an AnyRootedStickerball. Two facts let $T$ be glued cleanly: $\gamma$ is a boundary
   336	triangle of $M'$ lying in a \emph{unique} remainder tetrahedron $t_0$ (tautness pins it,
   337	$\text{faceCount}(\gamma)=1$; \code{degree3\_hanchor}), and $v$ is a vertex absent from $M'$
   338	(\code{degree3\_apex\_notMem\_*}), so adjoining $T$ along the single face $\gamma$ satisfies
   339	(a)--(c3) (the apex $v$ is new, so its link certificates are vacuous). Now assemble, given
   340	$t_\star$:
   341	\begin{itemize}
   342	\item if $t_\star\neq T$: take a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at $t_\star$ (induction) and
   343	append $T$ last;
   344	\item if $t_\star=T$: start with $T$, then a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at the anchor
   345	$t_0$ (induction), which glues to $T$ along $\gamma$.
   346	\end{itemize}
   347	Either way $M$ has a clean shelling rooted at $t_\star$.
   348	
   349	\medskip
   350	\noindent\textbf{No degree-$3$ vertex ($\card{\sigma}\ge 5$).} Pick a tetrahedron $e$ of $M$
   351	with exactly two faces on $\sigma$ (its existence is a count: with no degree-$3$ vertex there
   352	are more boundary triangles than tetrahedra; \code{exists\_eligibleTet}). Let $f_3,f_4$ be its
   353	two interior faces and $f_3\cap f_4$ its \emph{flip edge}. Remove $e$.
   354	\begin{itemize}
   355	\item \emph{Flip edge absent.} Exposing $f_3,f_4$ is a $2$--$2$ flip of the boundary: the new
   356	boundary is again a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ on the same vertices
   357	(\code{isSphere2\_flipBoundary\_of\_eligible}) and $M-e$ is a taut filling of it with fewer
   358	tetrahedra. By induction $M-e$ is an AnyRootedStickerball; re-gluing $e$ (two shared faces)
   359	is a clean glue step, its shared faces clean by the rule-out of Theorem~\ref{thm:pm}.
   360	\item \emph{Flip edge present.} Then $f_3\cap f_4\in\sigma$, and $\sigma$ separates along this
   361	edge into two $2$-spheres $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$; correspondingly $M-e$ splits along this edge into
   362	two taut sub-fillings $M_1,M_2$ with fewer tetrahedra (\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_sets},
   363	\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_algebra}). This is a decomposition of the same filter-by-side shape
   364	as Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, but a \emph{separate} argument: it is built directly on the cut
   365	(Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}/\code{exists\_cut}), not through \code{IsTaut.splits}.
   366	By induction each is an AnyRootedStickerball; concatenate their clean shellings --- rooting
   367	the second at the tetrahedron adjacent to the bridge --- and insert $e$ to bridge them
   368	(\code{glueStep\_bridge\_left}, \code{glueStep\_bridge\_right}).
   369	\end{itemize}
   370	In both sub-cases, choosing the root of the smaller piece(s) appropriately (and, if
   371	$t_\star=e$, starting with $e$) yields a clean shelling of $M$ rooted at $t_\star$.
   372	
   373	\medskip
   374	This proves the inductive invariant for $M$, completing the induction.
   375	\end{proof}
   376	
   377	\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}]
   378	Theorem~\ref{thm:freely} gives a clean shelling rooted at each tetrahedron; with nonempty
   379	boundary this is the AnyRootedStickerball conclusion. Each re-gluing above is a clean glue
   380	step, so the link certificates (c1)--(c3) hold at every step; the assembled shelling is
   381	therefore a genuine clean shelling and the rooted structure is preserved through the
   382	assembly.
   383	\end{proof}
   384	
   385	\begin{remark}[Where each ingredient is used]
   386	The recursion always lands on strictly smaller taut fillings of strictly smaller spheres,
   387	so the induction terminates. Tautness is used (i) to make every cut-restriction again taut,
   388	so the inductive hypothesis applies (Theorem~\ref{thm:split} for the degree-$3$ cut, the
   389	analogous side-decomposition for the prime case); (ii) through
   390	Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} to keep every intermediate complex triangle-bounded, which the prime
   391	rule-out needs; and (iii) to pin boundary triangles into unique tetrahedra, giving the
   392	gluing anchors. The rooted structure is preserved because every assembly step is a
   393	\emph{clean} glue step.
   394	\end{remark}
   395	
   396	%==========================================================================
   397	\section{Lean correspondence and status}\label{sec:status}
   398	
   399	The development builds successfully; a search of \code{lean/Taut} finds no \code{sorry},
   400	\code{admit}, \code{native\_decide}, \code{axiom}, or \code{unsafe}. Each theorem below
   401	reduces to the three foundational axioms \code{propext}, \code{Classical.choice},
   402	\code{Quot.sound} only.
   403	
   404	\begin{center}\small
   405	\begin{tabular}{lll}
   406	\hline
   407	Result here & Lean identifier & Status\\
   408	\hline
   409	Lem~\ref{lem:cut} ($H_1=0$, cut) & \code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}, \code{exists\_cut} & fully proved\\
   410	Thm~\ref{thm:add} (additivity) & \code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} & fully proved\\
   411	Thm~\ref{thm:split} (splitting) & \code{IsTaut.splits\_full} & fully proved\\
   412	Thm~\ref{thm:pm} (pure, triangle-bounded) & \code{taut\_isPseudomanifold} & fully proved\\
   413	Thm~\ref{thm:freely} (rooted at each tet) & \code{theorem3\_clean} & fully proved\\
   414	Thm~\ref{thm:anyrooted} (AnyRootedStickerball) & \code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball} & fully proved\\
   415	rational splitting/additivity & \code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}, \code{Qvol\_add\_*} & fully proved\\
   416	\hline
   417	\end{tabular}
   418	\end{center}
   419	
   420	\noindent\emph{Definitions weaker than their classical namesakes.}
   421	\begin{itemize}
   422	\item \emph{Taut} (\code{IsTaut}) is combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality
   423	(Definition~\ref{def:taut}), not a geometric condition.
   424	\item \emph{Triangle-bounded} (\code{IsPseudomanifold} in the development) means only ``every
   425	triangle in at most two tetrahedra.'' It is \emph{not} a full pseudomanifold; normality
   426	(link-connectedness) is established separately, via the clean shelling (see
   427	\S\ref{sec:classical}). Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} proves the triangle bound only.
   428	\item \emph{Clean shelling} (\code{IsCleanShelling}) carries the link certificates
   429	(c1)--(c3) as data; this is what makes it certify a clean $3$-complex, and is stronger than a
   430	boundary-only shelling.
   431	\end{itemize}
   432	
   433	%==========================================================================
   434	\section{Relation to the classical statement; remaining bridges}\label{sec:classical}
   435	
   436	The conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} is stated in Lean-backed terms
   437	(AnyRootedStickerball). Two questions separate this from a classical ``shellable normal
   438	$3$-ball'' statement.
   439	
   440	\paragraph{Proved bridge: AnyRootedStickerball $\Rightarrow$ normal $3$-pseudomanifold.}
   441	The development proves that a stickerball is a clean $3$-complex:
   442	\[
   443	\code{IsStickerball.isClean3Complex}\colon\quad
   444	\text{stickerball}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{pure}\ \wedge\ \text{triangle-bounded}\ \wedge\
   445	\text{Normal3},
   446	\]
   447	where \code{Normal3} is \emph{edge-link connected and vertex-link connected}
   448	(\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball.isClean3Complex} for the AnyRooted form). This is proved by
   449	iterating the single-insertion lemma \code{clean3Complex\_insert} along the clean shelling;
   450	the three certificates (c1)--(c3) of each glue step supply exactly the triangle-count,
   451	edge-link, and vertex-link preservation (\code{isPseudomanifold\_insert},
   452	\code{edgeLinkConnected\_insert}, \code{vertexLinkConnected\_insert}). Thus pseudomanifoldness
   453	(triangle bound), edge-link connectedness, vertex-link connectedness, and nonempty boundary
   454	are all \emph{Lean-proved} consequences of the conclusion; the corresponding corollaries are
   455	\code{taut\_filling\_is\_clean3Complex} and \code{taut\_filling\_is\_shellable}.
   456	
   457	\paragraph{Missing bridge: stickerball $\Rightarrow$ topological/PL $3$-ball.}
   458	The statement ``a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold with nonempty boundary is
   459	(PL-)homeomorphic to the $3$-ball'' is the classical shelling theorem of Danaraj--Klee /
   460	Bj\"orner. \emph{No bridge theorem from \code{IsStickerball} to a topological or PL ball
   461	appears in the current development}; it is recorded only as a docstring remark. Any classical
   462	reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} as ``$M$ triangulates a $3$-ball'' must invoke that
   463	external theorem explicitly. In the present, fully formalized terms the conclusion is exactly:
   464	\emph{the tetrahedra of a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere form an
   465	AnyRootedStickerball --- a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold, with boundary the given
   466	sphere, that can be clean-shelled starting from any tetrahedron}.
   467	
   468	\paragraph{Hypotheses to carry into a paper statement.}
   469	Chains are simplicial and the boundary cycle is $\pm1$ on $\sigma$
   470	(\code{SimplicialChain}, \code{UnitOn}); splitting (Theorem~\ref{thm:split}) needs dimension
   471	$n\ge 2$ while additivity (Theorem~\ref{thm:add}) needs only $n\ge 1$; AlmostDisjointness is
   472	$\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$; the ambient $V$ is infinite; and \code{IsSphere2} includes the Euler
   473	relation $V+F=E+2$ as part of its definition, from which $H_1=0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}) is
   474	derived rather than assumed.
   475	
   476	\end{document}

    83	
    84	/-- A combinatorial closed surface: pure 2-dimensional, every edge in exactly
    85	two faces, connected vertex links, connected. This is `IsSphere2` minus the
    86	`χ = 2` constraint — the geometric content the homology machinery (b₂ = 1,
    87	r₁ = V−1) actually rests on. Euler enters `IsSphere2` only via nonemptiness. -/
    88	structure IsClosedSurface (σ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop where
    89	  pure : ∀ f ∈ σ, f.card = 3
    90	  closed : ∀ e ∈ edgesOf σ, edgeDeg σ e = 2
    91	  linkConn : ∀ v ∈ vertsOf σ, ConnOn (linkGraph σ v) (linkVerts σ v)
    92	  conn : ConnOn (skel σ) (vertsOf σ)
    93	
    94	/-- A combinatorial 2-sphere: a closed surface with Euler characteristic 2.
    95	This is the paper's "clean complex" (normal pseudomanifold) sharpened by
    96	`χ = 2`, replacing "simplicial triangulation of S²". -/
    97	structure IsSphere2 (σ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop where
    98	  pure : ∀ f ∈ σ, f.card = 3
    99	  closed : ∀ e ∈ edgesOf σ, edgeDeg σ e = 2
   100	  linkConn : ∀ v ∈ vertsOf σ, ConnOn (linkGraph σ v) (linkVerts σ v)
   101	  conn : ConnOn (skel σ) (vertsOf σ)
   102	  euler : (vertsOf σ).card + σ.card = (edgesOf σ).card + 2
   103

    30	boundary triangle), it carries the *quantitative* clean-insert certificates that
    31	`clean3Complex_insert` consumes.  These do **not** follow from `clean` alone, so

    33	structure CleanGlueStep (t : Finset V) (τ B B' : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop where
    34	  /-- the underlying boundary glue step (one or two shared faces, symmetric difference). -/
    35	  weak : BoundaryGlueStep t B B'
    36	  /-- no rogue old face: every face of `t` already in `τ` lies in a shared boundary triangle. -/
    37	  clean : ∀ f, f ⊆ t → (∃ s ∈ τ, f ⊆ s) → ∃ g ∈ tetFaces t ∩ B, f ⊆ g
    38	  /-- `t` is fresh (rules out a doubled tet). -/
    39	  newTet : t ∉ τ
    40	  /-- each triangle of `t` lies in ≤ 1 old tet (so triangle-count stays ≤ 2). -/
    41	  hpmc : ∀ f, f.card = 3 → f ⊆ t → faceCount τ f ≤ 1
    42	  /-- each edge of `t` is new to `τ` or already shares an apex with `τ`'s edge-link there. -/
    43	  helc : ∀ e, e ⊆ t → e.card = 2 →
    44	    edgeLinkVerts τ e = ∅ ∨ ((t \ e) ∩ edgeLinkVerts τ e).Nonempty
    45	  /-- each vertex of `t` is new to `τ` or already shares an apex with `τ`'s vertex-link there. -/
    46	  hvlc : ∀ v ∈ t, vertexLinkVerts τ v = ∅ ∨
    47	    ((t \ {v}) ∩ vertexLinkVerts τ v).Nonempty
    48	
    49	/-- Clean shelling from boundary `B₀`, **threading the accumulated tet-set `τ`**:
    50	each step glues onto the set built so far, then recurses on `insert t τ`. -/
    51	def CleanShellFrom :
    52	    Finset (Finset V) → Finset (Finset V) → List (Finset V) → Finset (Finset V) → Prop
    53	  | _, B₀, [],     B => B = B₀
    54	  | τ, B₀, t :: l, B => ∃ B₁, CleanGlueStep t τ B₀ B₁ ∧ CleanShellFrom (insert t τ) B₁ l B
    55	
    56	/-- A clean shelling with final boundary `B`: the head tet is the initial
    57	tetrahedron (accumulated set `{t}`, boundary `tetFaces t`, no glue step), and the
    58	rest are glued on cleanly in order. -/
    59	def IsCleanShelling : List (Finset V) → Finset (Finset V) → Prop
    60	  | [],     _ => False
    61	  | t :: l, B => t.card = 4 ∧ CleanShellFrom {t} (tetFaces t) l B
    62	
    63	/-- A *clean ball*: a tet-set admitting a clean shelling (a nodup ordering of its
    64	tets gluing up cleanly to boundary `B`). -/
    65	def IsCleanBall (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    66	  ∃ l : List (Finset V), l.toFinset = τ ∧ l.Nodup ∧ IsCleanShelling l B
    67	
    68	/-- *Freely clean shellable*: **any** tet can serve as the head of a clean
    69	shelling.  This is the public free-sticker-ball predicate Theorem 3 exports. -/
    70	def FreelyCleanShellable (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    71	  ∀ t ∈ τ, ∃ l : List (Finset V),
    72	    l.head? = some t ∧ l.toFinset = τ ∧ l.Nodup ∧ IsCleanShelling l B
    73	
    74	/-! ## Stickerball predicates (the combinatorial B³ certificate) -/
    75	
    76	/-- A **stickerball**: a shellable clean 3-complex with nonempty boundary.  Concretely `IsCleanBall`
    77	(a clean shelling exists — building *up* by sticking tetrahedra on, the reverse of shucking down) with
    78	a nonempty boundary `B`.  Being an `IsCleanBall` it is in particular an `IsClean3Complex`
    79	(pure, every triangle in ≤ 2 tets, connected vertex *and* edge links) — see `IsStickerball.isClean3Complex`.
    80	
    81	By the standard PL theorem that a shellable normal 3-pseudomanifold with nonempty boundary is a PL
    82	ball, this is the combinatorial certificate for a triangulation of `B³`.  This project proves the
    83	combinatorial certificate; it does **not** formalize that external PL-homeomorphism theorem (see
    84	`notes/future-projects.md`). -/
    85	def IsStickerball (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    86	  IsCleanBall τ B ∧ B.Nonempty
    87	
    88	/-- An **anyrooted stickerball** is a stickerball whose shelling may start with any prescribed
    89	tetrahedron (`FreelyCleanShellable`).  "Stickerball" by itself is *not* anyrooted — that is the
    90	strengthened property. -/
    91	def IsAnyrootedStickerball (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    92	  IsStickerball τ B ∧ FreelyCleanShellable τ B
    93	
    94	lemma IsAnyrootedStickerball.toStickerball {τ B : Finset (Finset V)}
    95	    (h : IsAnyrootedStickerball τ B) : IsStickerball τ B := h.1
    96	
    97	lemma IsAnyrootedStickerball.freelyCleanShellable {τ B : Finset (Finset V)}
    98	    (h : IsAnyrootedStickerball τ B) : FreelyCleanShellable τ B := h.2

     4	/-!
     5	# Pseudomanifold invariant — the "simplicial" half of a sticker ball
     6	
     7	`FreelyShellable`/`IsBall` originally tracked only the shelling (boundary by
     8	symmetric difference), which admits non-simplicial configurations: a triangle
     9	can be re-exposed into a third tet (an edge "flipped back and forth").  A sticker
    10	ball *for us* is a shellable **simplicial** complex, so we carry the
    11	pseudomanifold condition — every triangle lies in at most two tets — as part of
    12	the invariant.  This file sets up the face-incidence bookkeeping.
    13	-/
    14	
    15	namespace Taut
    16	
    17	open Finset
    18	
    19	variable {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V]
    20	
    21	/-- Number of tets of `τ` that contain the face `f`. -/
    22	def faceCount (τ : Finset (Finset V)) (f : Finset V) : ℕ :=
    23	  (τ.filter (fun t => f ⊆ t)).card
    24	
    25	/-- `τ` is a 3-pseudomanifold: every triangle lies in at most two tets.
    26	With a unit boundary this forces every boundary triangle into exactly one tet
    27	(two would cancel to `0` or sum to `±2`, never the boundary value `±1`). -/
    28	def IsPseudomanifold (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    29	  ∀ f, f.card = 3 → faceCount τ f ≤ 2
    30	
    31	/-- `TriangleBounded3` — the triangle-count condition (`IsPseudomanifold`): every
    32	triangle lies in at most two tets.  This is *not* a normal/clean pseudomanifold:
    33	it admits two balls joined along an edge, or along a single vertex. -/
    34	abbrev TriangleBounded3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := IsPseudomanifold τ
    35	
    36	/-- A face contained in no tet has incidence zero — used to discharge the
    37	triangle-cleanliness hypothesis from the triangle rule-out (`s₁,s₂ ∉ M−t`). -/
    38	lemma faceCount_eq_zero {τ : Finset (Finset V)} {f : Finset V}
    39	    (h : ∀ t ∈ τ, ¬ f ⊆ t) : faceCount τ f = 0 := by
    40	  unfold faceCount
    41	  rw [Finset.filter_false_of_mem h]; rfl
    42	
    43	lemma faceCount_insert_of_not_mem {τ : Finset (Finset V)} {t : Finset V}
    44	    (ht : t ∉ τ) (f : Finset V) :
    45	    faceCount (insert t τ) f = faceCount τ f + (if f ⊆ t then 1 else 0) := by
    46	  unfold faceCount
    47	  rw [Finset.filter_insert]
    48	  by_cases h : f ⊆ t
    49	  · have hnm : t ∉ τ.filter (fun s => f ⊆ s) :=
    50	      fun hmem => ht (Finset.mem_of_mem_filter t hmem)
    51	    simp only [if_pos h, Finset.card_insert_of_notMem hnm]
    52	  · simp only [if_neg h, Nat.add_zero]
    53	
    54	lemma isPseudomanifold_singleton (t : Finset V) :
    55	    IsPseudomanifold ({t} : Finset (Finset V)) := by
    56	  intro f _
    57	  have h : faceCount ({t} : Finset (Finset V)) f ≤ 1 := by
    58	    unfold faceCount
    59	    have hle := Finset.card_filter_le ({t} : Finset (Finset V)) (fun s => f ⊆ s)
    60	    simpa using hle
    61	  omega
    62	
    63	/-- **Clean glue preserves the pseudomanifold property.** Inserting a fresh tet
    64	`t` whose every *triangle* lies in at most one existing tet keeps `τ` a
    65	pseudomanifold.  The hypothesis `hclean` is exactly the "no re-exposure"
    66	condition the forward construction must supply at each shelling step. -/
    67	lemma isPseudomanifold_insert {τ : Finset (Finset V)} {t : Finset V}
    68	    (hτ : IsPseudomanifold τ) (ht : t ∉ τ)
    69	    (hclean : ∀ f, f.card = 3 → f ⊆ t → faceCount τ f ≤ 1) :
    70	    IsPseudomanifold (insert t τ) := by
    71	  intro f hf
    72	  rw [faceCount_insert_of_not_mem ht]
    73	  by_cases h : f ⊆ t
    74	  · rw [if_pos h]; have := hclean f hf h; omega
    75	  · rw [if_neg h]; have := hτ f hf; omega

    72	/-! ## Zvol and tautness -/
    73	
    74	/-- The filling volume: the least L¹ norm of a chain with boundary `X`. -/
    75	noncomputable def Zvol (X : Chain V) : ℕ :=
    76	  sInf {n | ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = n}
    77	
    78	/-- `M` is an optimal filling of its own boundary. -/
    79	def IsTaut (M : Chain V) : Prop := nrm M = Zvol (bdry M)
    80	
    81	lemma Zvol_le {X M : Chain V} (h : bdry M = X) : Zvol X ≤ nrm M :=
    82	  Nat.sInf_le ⟨M, h, rfl⟩

    85	    bdry (cone x X) = X := by
    86	  have h := bdry_cone_add_cone_bdry x X

    88	  exact h

     1	import Mathlib
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Simplicial chains on a full simplex
     5	
     6	Foundation for formalizing Doyle–Ellison–Wang, "Taut fillings".
     7	
     8	We work with the AUGMENTED, dimension-mixed simplicial chain group of the
     9	full simplex on a linearly ordered vertex type `V`: a chain is a finitely
    10	supported function `Finset V →₀ ℤ`, a generator being an unoriented simplex
    11	`s : Finset V` carrying its canonical orientation (vertices in increasing
    12	order).  `∅` is the (−1)-simplex; its presence makes the contracting
    13	homotopy `∂ ∘ cone + cone ∘ ∂ = id` exact and global, which is what gives
    14	filling-existence for closed chains.
    15	
    16	Sign bookkeeping is via `cnt x s = #{y ∈ s | y < x}` and
    17	`sgn x s = (-1) ^ cnt x s`: the `i`-th vertex of a sorted simplex has
    18	exactly `i` smaller members, so `sgn` is the classical `(-1)^i`.
    19	
    20	Main definitions: `bdry` (boundary), `cone`, `lk` (link/strip), `nbhd`
    21	(restriction to simplices containing a vertex), `vert`, `nrm` (L¹ norm),
    22	`deg`, `SubChain`.
    23	
    24	Main results:
    25	* `bdry_bdry` : ∂∂ = 0;
    26	* `bdry_cone_add_cone_bdry` : ∂(cone x M) + cone x (∂M) = M;
    27	* `cone_lk` : cone x (lk x M) = nbhd x M;
    28	* `nrm_cone_add_deg` : nrm (cone x M) + deg x M = nrm M.
    29	-/
    30	
    31	namespace Taut
    32	
    33	variable {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V]
    34	
    35	/-- Augmented, dimension-mixed simplicial chains on the full simplex with
    36	vertex type `V`. -/
    37	abbrev Chain (V : Type*) := Finset V →₀ ℤ
    38	
    39	/-! ## Counting below, and signs -/
    40	
    41	/-- The number of vertices of `s` strictly below `x`: the position `x` takes
    42	(or would take) in the sorted listing of `insert x s`. -/
    43	def cnt (x : V) (s : Finset V) : ℕ := (s.filter (· < x)).card
    44	
    45	/-- The sign `(-1) ^ cnt x s`: the sign of the permutation sorting `x`
    46	into `s`. -/
    47	def sgn (x : V) (s : Finset V) : ℤ := (-1) ^ cnt x s
    48	
    49	lemma sgn_mul_self (x : V) (s : Finset V) : sgn x s * sgn x s = 1 := by
    50	  simp only [sgn, ← pow_add]
    51	  exact Even.neg_one_pow ⟨_, rfl⟩
    52	
    53	lemma natAbs_sgn (x : V) (s : Finset V) : (sgn x s).natAbs = 1 := by
    54	  simp [sgn, Int.natAbs_pow]
    55	
    56	lemma sgn_smul_sgn_smul (x : V) (s : Finset V) (M : Chain V) :
    57	    sgn x s • sgn x s • M = M := by
    58	  rw [smul_smul, sgn_mul_self, one_smul]
    59	
    60	lemma cnt_insert (x z : V) (t : Finset V) (hz : z ∉ t) :

    24	/-- A chain is *simplicial* if every coefficient is `-1`, `0`, or `1` — i.e. it
    25	is a genuine multiset of oriented simplices with no repeats. A taut filling that
    26	"arises from a triangulation" is such a chain on the tets of a ball. -/
    27	def SimplicialChain (M : Chain V) : Prop :=
    28	  ∀ t, M t = -1 ∨ M t = 0 ∨ M t = 1
    29	
    30	lemma SimplicialChain.natAbs_eq_one {M : Chain V} (h : SimplicialChain M) {s : Finset V}
    31	    (hs : s ∈ M.support) : (M s).natAbs = 1 := by
    32	  have hne : M s ≠ 0 := Finsupp.mem_support_iff.mp hs
    33	  rcases h s with h1 | h0 | h1
    34	  · rw [h1]; decide
    35	  · exact absurd h0 hne
    36	  · rw [h1]; decide
    37	
    38	/-- **The norm/support bridge.** For a simplicial chain the norm `|M|` counts
    39	its support: `nrm M = #(M.support)`. This is what lets the induction on `|M|`
    40	(`nrm`) talk to the ball's facet count (`M.support.card`). -/

   342	/-- **Rational splitting (Corollary 1, step 6 endpoint).** A taut rational
   343	filling `M` of a sum `X + Y` (with `X` supported on `A`, `Y` on `B`, both cycles,
   344	and a small intersection) splits along the predicate `· ⊆ A` into a taut filling
   345	of `X` and a taut filling of `Y`.  Proved by clearing denominators to the
   346	committed integer endpoint `IsTaut.splits_full` and dividing back. -/
   347	theorem IsQTaut.splits_full {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V] [Infinite V]
   348	    {A B : Finset V} {n : ℕ} (hn : 2 ≤ n) (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1)
   349	    {X Y : QChain V}
   350	    (hX : ∀ s ∈ X.support, s ⊆ A ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   351	    (hY : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   352	    (hXc : Qbdry X = 0) (hYc : Qbdry Y = 0)
   353	    {M : QChain V} (hMt : IsQTaut M) (hM1 : Qbdry M = X + Y) :
   354	    Qbdry (M.filter fun t => t ⊆ A) = X ∧ Qbdry (M.filter fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = Y ∧
   355	    IsQTaut (M.filter fun t => t ⊆ A) ∧ IsQTaut (M.filter fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) ∧
   356	    M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A) + M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = M := by
   357	  classical
   358	  obtain ⟨q, hq, hMint, hXint, hYint⟩ := exists_nat_smul_integral_three M X Y
   359	  have hqQ : (q : ℚ) ≠ 0 := by exact_mod_cast hq.ne'
   360	  set K : Chain V := QChain.toInt ((q : ℚ) • M) hMint with hKdef
   361	  set KX : Chain V := QChain.toInt ((q : ℚ) • X) hXint with hKXdef
   362	  set KY : Chain V := QChain.toInt ((q : ℚ) • Y) hYint with hKYdef
   363	  have hKM : intToQChain K = (q : ℚ) • M := intToQChain_toInt _ hMint
   364	  have hKX : intToQChain KX = (q : ℚ) • X := intToQChain_toInt _ hXint
   365	  have hKY : intToQChain KY = (q : ℚ) • Y := intToQChain_toInt _ hYint
   366	  have hKXc : bdry KX = 0 := by
   367	    apply intToQChain_injective
   368	    rw [intToQChain_zero, ← Qbdry_intToQChain, hKX, map_smul, hXc, smul_zero]
   369	  have hKYc : bdry KY = 0 := by
   370	    apply intToQChain_injective
   371	    rw [intToQChain_zero, ← Qbdry_intToQChain, hKY, map_smul, hYc, smul_zero]
   372	  have hKbdry : bdry K = KX + KY := by
   373	    apply intToQChain_injective
   374	    rw [← Qbdry_intToQChain, hKM, map_smul, hM1, smul_add, intToQChain_add, hKX,
   375	      hKY]
   376	  have hKXsupp : KX.support = X.support := by
   377	    rw [← support_intToQChain KX, hKX, support_nat_smul_of_pos hq]
   378	  have hKYsupp : KY.support = Y.support := by
   379	    rw [← support_intToQChain KY, hKY, support_nat_smul_of_pos hq]
   380	  have hX' : ∀ s ∈ KX.support, s ⊆ A ∧ s.card = n + 1 := by
   381	    rw [hKXsupp]; exact hX
   382	  have hY' : ∀ s ∈ KY.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1 := by
   383	    rw [hKYsupp]; exact hY
   384	  have hKtaut : IsTaut K := IsQTaut.toInt_nat_smul hMt hq hMint
   385	  obtain ⟨hbA, hbB, _, _, _⟩ :=
   386	    IsTaut.splits_full hn hC hX' hY' hKXc hKYc hKtaut hKbdry
   387	  have hQbA : Qbdry (M.filter fun t => t ⊆ A) = X := by
   388	    apply smul_right_injective (QChain V) hqQ
   389	    show (q : ℚ) • Qbdry (M.filter fun t => t ⊆ A) = (q : ℚ) • X
   390	    rw [← map_smul, ← QChain.filter_smul, ← hKM, ← intToQChain_filter,
   391	      Qbdry_intToQChain, hbA, hKX]
   392	  have hQbB : Qbdry (M.filter fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = Y := by
   393	    apply smul_right_injective (QChain V) hqQ
   394	    show (q : ℚ) • Qbdry (M.filter fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = (q : ℚ) • Y
   395	    rw [← map_smul, ← QChain.filter_smul, ← hKM, ← intToQChain_filter,
   396	      Qbdry_intToQChain, hbB, hKY]
   397	  have hMsum : M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A) + M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) = M :=
   398	    Finsupp.filter_pos_add_filter_neg M _
   399	  set MA : QChain V := M.filter (fun t => t ⊆ A) with hMAdef
   400	  set MB : QChain V := M.filter (fun t => ¬ t ⊆ A) with hMBdef
   401	  have hnorm : Qnrm MA + Qnrm MB = Qnrm M :=
   402	    Qnrm_filter_add_Qnrm_filter_neg _ M
   403	  have hMtaut_A : IsQTaut MA := by
   404	    intro N hN
   405	    have hbdryN : Qbdry (N + MB) = Qbdry M := by
   406	      rw [map_add, hN, hMAdef, hMBdef, hQbA, hQbB, hM1]
   407	    have hle : Qnrm M ≤ Qnrm (N + MB) := hMt (N + MB) hbdryN
   408	    have htri : Qnrm (N + MB) ≤ Qnrm N + Qnrm MB := Qnrm_add_le N MB
   409	    have : Qnrm M ≤ Qnrm N + Qnrm MB := le_trans hle htri
   410	    rw [← hnorm] at this
   411	    exact le_of_add_le_add_right this
   412	  have hMtaut_B : IsQTaut MB := by
   413	    intro N hN
   414	    have hbdryN : Qbdry (MA + N) = Qbdry M := by
   415	      rw [map_add, hN, hMAdef, hMBdef, hQbA, hQbB, hM1]
   416	    have hle : Qnrm M ≤ Qnrm (MA + N) := hMt (MA + N) hbdryN
   417	    have htri : Qnrm (MA + N) ≤ Qnrm MA + Qnrm N := Qnrm_add_le MA N
   418	    have : Qnrm M ≤ Qnrm MA + Qnrm N := le_trans hle htri
   419	    rw [← hnorm] at this
   420	    exact le_of_add_le_add_left this
   421	  exact ⟨hQbA, hQbB, hMtaut_A, hMtaut_B, hMsum⟩

   423	/-! ## The rational chain homotopy
   424	
   425	The ℚ-coefficient analogues of `bdry_bdry` and `bdry_cone_add_cone_bdry`
   426	(`Taut/Chains.lean`).  The cone operator `Qcone x` is a contracting homotopy of
   427	the augmented full simplex over ℚ, so every closed rational chain is filled by
   428	its cone (`Qbdry_Qcone_of_closed`). -/

     3	/-!
     4	# Corollary 1: rational filling foundations
     5	
     6	Rational analogue of the integral chain machinery in `Taut/Chains.lean`.
     7	`Qnrm` is real-valued so the filling-volume infimum lives in a conditionally
     8	complete order.
     9	-/
    10	
    11	namespace Taut
    12	
    13	open Finset
    14	
    15	variable {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V]
    16	
    17	abbrev QChain (V : Type*) := Finset V →₀ ℚ
    18	
    19	/-- Boundary of one generator over ℚ (mirrors `bdryGen`). -/
    20	noncomputable def QbdryGen (s : Finset V) : QChain V :=
    21	  ∑ x ∈ s, (sgn x s : ℚ) • Finsupp.single (s.erase x) (1 : ℚ)
    22	
    23	noncomputable def Qbdry : QChain V →ₗ[ℚ] QChain V :=
    24	  Finsupp.lsum ℚ fun s => LinearMap.toSpanSingleton ℚ (QChain V) (QbdryGen s)
    25	
    26	@[simp] lemma Qbdry_single (s : Finset V) (c : ℚ) :
    27	    Qbdry (Finsupp.single s c) = c • QbdryGen s := by
    28	  simp [Qbdry, Finsupp.lsum_single, LinearMap.toSpanSingleton_apply]
    29	
    30	/-- Cone of one generator over ℚ (mirrors `coneGen`). -/
    31	noncomputable def QconeGen (x : V) (s : Finset V) : QChain V :=
    32	  if x ∈ s then 0 else (sgn x s : ℚ) • Finsupp.single (insert x s) (1 : ℚ)
    33	
    34	noncomputable def Qcone (x : V) : QChain V →ₗ[ℚ] QChain V :=
    35	  Finsupp.lsum ℚ fun s => LinearMap.toSpanSingleton ℚ (QChain V) (QconeGen x s)
    36	
    37	@[simp] lemma Qcone_single (x : V) (s : Finset V) (c : ℚ) :
    38	    Qcone x (Finsupp.single s c) = c • QconeGen x s := by
    39	  simp [Qcone, Finsupp.lsum_single, LinearMap.toSpanSingleton_apply]
    40	
    41	noncomputable def Qnrm (M : QChain V) : ℝ := ∑ s ∈ M.support, |(M s : ℝ)|
    42	
    43	noncomputable def QvolSet (X : QChain V) : Set ℝ :=
    44	  {r | ∃ M : QChain V, Qbdry M = X ∧ Qnrm M = r}
    45	
    46	noncomputable def Qvol (X : QChain V) : ℝ := sInf (QvolSet X)
    47	
    48	/-- `M` is order-optimal, NOT `Qnrm = Qvol` — this avoids needing rational
    49	minimisers to exist. -/
    50	def IsQTaut (M : QChain V) : Prop := ∀ N : QChain V, Qbdry N = Qbdry M → Qnrm M ≤ Qnrm N
    51	
    52	def QChain.IsIntegral (M : QChain V) : Prop := ∀ s, ∃ z : ℤ, M s = (z : ℚ)
    53	
    54	/-- **Clearing denominators.** Every rational chain becomes integral after
    55	scaling by a positive natural number (the lcm of its coefficients'
    56	denominators). -/
    57	lemma exists_nat_smul_integral (M : QChain V) :
    58	    ∃ q : ℕ, 0 < q ∧ QChain.IsIntegral ((q : ℚ) • M) := by
    59	  classical
    60	  refine ⟨M.support.lcm (fun s => (M s).den), ?_, ?_⟩
    61	  · refine Nat.pos_of_ne_zero ?_
    62	    rw [Finset.lcm_ne_zero_iff]
    63	    intro s _
    64	    exact (M s).den_ne_zero
    65	  · intro s
    66	    rw [Finsupp.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul]
    67	    by_cases hs : s ∈ M.support
    68	    · set q : ℕ := M.support.lcm (fun s => (M s).den) with hq
    69	      have hdvd : (M s).den ∣ q := Finset.dvd_lcm hs
    70	      refine ⟨((q / (M s).den : ℕ) : ℤ) * (M s).num, ?_⟩
    71	      have hden : ((M s).den : ℚ) ≠ 0 := by exact_mod_cast (M s).den_ne_zero
    72	      have hcancel : (q / (M s).den) * (M s).den = q := Nat.div_mul_cancel hdvd
    73	      have hqcast : (q : ℚ) = ((q / (M s).den : ℕ) : ℚ) * ((M s).den : ℚ) := by
    74	        rw [← Nat.cast_mul, hcancel]
    75	      have hmul : ((M s).den : ℚ) * M s = ((M s).num : ℚ) := by
    76	        have h := Rat.num_div_den (M s)
    77	        rw [div_eq_iff hden] at h
    78	        rw [h, mul_comm]
    79	      rw [hqcast, mul_assoc, hmul, Int.cast_mul, Int.cast_natCast]
    80	    · rw [Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.mp hs, mul_zero]
    81	      exact ⟨0, by push_cast; ring⟩
    82

  3384	/-- **Theorem 2 (clean route)**: a taut filling of a combinatorial 2-sphere `σ` is a *clean ball*
  3385	(`IsCleanBall` = the paper's *stickerball* = a shellable clean ball: it admits a clean shelling).
  3386	
  3387	Two facts about this endpoint are worth making explicit (both are *named theorems*, not handwaves):
  3388	
  3389	* **It is genuinely clean, including connected vertex links.**  `IsCleanBall M.support σ` provably
  3390	  entails `Clean3Complex M.support` via `IsCleanBall.clean3Complex` — exported here as the standalone
  3391	  endpoint `taut_clean3Complex`.  `Clean3Complex = Pure3 ∧ TriangleBounded3 ∧ Normal3` and
  3392	  `Normal3 = EdgeLinkConnected ∧ VertexLinkConnected`, so connected vertex links (dim-0 links) and
  3393	  connected edge links (dim-1 links) — the paper's "normal" condition — are both delivered.  The
  3394	  per-step vertex-link compatibility is the `CleanGlueStep.hvlc` field, folded by
  3395	  `IsCleanShelling.clean3Complex`.
  3396	
  3397	* **The proof actually establishes the stronger free-start statement.**  `IsCleanBall` asks only for
  3398	  *one* clean shelling, but this theorem is a one-line weakening of `theorem3_clean`, which produces a
  3399	  clean shelling beginning with *any* prescribed tet (`FreelyCleanShellable`).  So the substance of
  3400	  Theorem 3 (free choice of starting tet) is already proved by the same minimal-counterexample
  3401	  induction; `theorem2_clean` simply forgets the head condition.  `IsCleanBall` (stickerball /
  3402	  shellable) and `FreelyCleanShellable` (free-start) are kept as *distinct* predicates on purpose —
  3403	  "stickerball" is not silently redefined to mean "freely shellable". -/

  1089	theorem flipEdgePresent_side_sets {σ : Finset (Finset V)} {X M : Chain V} {e f₃ f₄ : Finset V}
  1090	    (hσ : IsSphere2 σ)
  1091	    (hU : UnitOn X σ)
  1092	    (hXc : bdry X = 0)
  1093	    (hMX : bdry M = X)
  1094	    (hT : IsTaut M)
  1095	    (hS : SimplicialChain M)
  1096	    (he : EligibleTet M e)
  1097	    (hexp : exposedFaces M e = {f₃, f₄})
  1098	    (hf₃₄ : f₃ ≠ f₄)
  1099	    (hFlip : FlipEdgePresent σ f₃ f₄) :
  1100	    ∃ A B : Finset V,
  1101	      A ∩ B = f₃ ∩ f₄ ∧
  1102	      (f₃ ∩ f₄).card = 2 ∧
  1103	      IsSphere2 ((flipBoundary σ M e).filter (fun f => f ⊆ A)) ∧
  1104	      IsSphere2 ((flipBoundary σ M e).filter (fun f => f ⊆ B)) ∧
  1105	      (∀ t ∈ (removeTet M e).support, t ⊆ A ∨ t ⊆ B) ∧
  1106	      (∀ t ∈ (removeTet M e).support,
  1107	        (t ⊆ A ∧ ¬ t ⊆ B) ∨ (t ⊆ B ∧ ¬ t ⊆ A)) ∧
  1108	      f₃ ⊆ A ∧ ¬ f₃ ⊆ B ∧ f₄ ⊆ B ∧ ¬ f₄ ⊆ A ∧
  1109	      (∀ f ∈ flipBoundary σ M e, f ⊆ A ∨ f ⊆ B) := by
  1110	  rcases flipEdgePresent_boundary_side_package hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS he hexp hf₃₄ hFlip with
  1111	    ⟨A, B, hAB, hcd, hsphereA, hsphereB, hfaceCover, hf₃A, hf₃notB, hf₄B, hf₄notA⟩
  1112	  rcases flipEdgePresent_removeTet_support_partition hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS he hexp hf₃₄ hFlip hAB hcd hfaceCover with ⟨hcover, hsep⟩
  1113	  exact ⟨A, B, hAB, hcd, hsphereA, hsphereB, hcover, hsep, hf₃A, hf₃notB, hf₄B, hf₄notA, hfaceCover⟩
  1114	
  1115	theorem bdry_filter_eq_filter_bdry_of_side_sep {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V] {M : Chain V} {A B : Finset V}
  1116	    (hpure : ∀ t ∈ M.support, t.card = 4)
  1117	    (hsep : ∀ t ∈ M.support,
  1118	      (t ⊆ A ∧ ¬ t ⊆ B) ∨ (t ⊆ B ∧ ¬ t ⊆ A))
  1320	coverage and exact-side separation, the filtered side chains are closed, taut,
  1321	simplicial, strictly smaller, disjoint, and decompose the original support. -/
  1322	theorem flipEdgePresent_side_algebra {σ : Finset (Finset V)} {X M : Chain V} {e f₃ f₄ A B : Finset V}
  1323	    (hσ : IsSphere2 σ)
  1324	    (hU : UnitOn X σ)
  1325	    (hXc : bdry X = 0)
  1326	    (hMX : bdry M = X)
  1327	    (hT : IsTaut M)
  1328	    (hS : SimplicialChain M)
  1329	    (he : EligibleTet M e)
  1330	    (hexp : exposedFaces M e = {f₃, f₄})
  1331	    (hf₃₄ : f₃ ≠ f₄)
  1332	    (hFlip : FlipEdgePresent σ f₃ f₄)
  1333	    (hAB : A ∩ B = f₃ ∩ f₄)
  1334	    (hcd : (f₃ ∩ f₄).card = 2)
  1335	    (hcover : ∀ t ∈ (removeTet M e).support, t ⊆ A ∨ t ⊆ B)
  1336	    (hsep : ∀ t ∈ (removeTet M e).support,
  1337	      (t ⊆ A ∧ ¬ t ⊆ B) ∨ (t ⊆ B ∧ ¬ t ⊆ A)) :
  1338	    UnitOn (bdry ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)))
  1339	        ((flipBoundary σ M e).filter (fun f => f ⊆ A)) ∧
  1340	      UnitOn (bdry ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)))
  1341	        ((flipBoundary σ M e).filter (fun f => f ⊆ B)) ∧
  1342	      bdry (bdry ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A))) = 0 ∧
  1343	      bdry (bdry ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B))) = 0 ∧
  1344	      IsTaut ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)) ∧
  1345	      IsTaut ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)) ∧
  1346	      SimplicialChain ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)) ∧
  1347	      SimplicialChain ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)) ∧
  1348	      nrm ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)) < nrm M ∧
  1349	      nrm ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)) < nrm M ∧
  1350	      e ∉ ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)).support ∧
  1351	      e ∉ ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)).support ∧
  1352	      Disjoint ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)).support
  1353	        ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)).support ∧
  1354	      M.support =
  1355	        insert e
  1356	          (((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ A)).support ∪
  1357	            ((removeTet M e).filter (fun t => t ⊆ B)).support) := by
  1358	  obtain ⟨hUA, hUB⟩ := flipEdgePresent_side_units hσ hU hMX hT hS he hAB hcd hsep
  1359	  obtain ⟨hTA, hTB, hSA, hSB, hnA, hnB, heA, heB⟩ := flipEdgePresent_side_taut_simp_norm (M := M) (e := e) (A := A) (B := B) hT hS he
  1360	  obtain ⟨hdisj, hsupp⟩ := flipEdgePresent_side_supports (M := M) (e := e) (A := A) (B := B) he hcover hsep
  1361	  refine ⟨hUA, hUB, ?_, ?_, hTA, hTB, hSA, hSB, hnA, hnB, heA, heB, hdisj, hsupp⟩
  1362	  · exact bdry_bdry _
  1363	  · exact bdry_bdry _

   139	theorem IsTaut.dim_pure {M : Chain V} (hM : IsTaut M) {k : ℕ}
   140	    (hbd : ∀ s ∈ (bdry M).support, s.card = k) :
   141	    ∀ s ∈ M.support, s.card = k + 1 := by

   100	def EdgeLinkConnected (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
   101	  ∀ e : Finset V, e.card = 2 → ConnOn (edgeLinkGraph τ e) (edgeLinkVerts τ e)

   403	/-- `Pure3 τ`: every tet has 4 vertices (genuine 3-dimensionality). -/
   404	def Pure3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := ∀ t ∈ τ, t.card = 4
   405	
   406	/-- `Normal3 τ`: connected edge links *and* connected vertex links — the normal
   407	(no-pinch) condition.  `TriangleBounded3 ∧ EdgeLinkConnected` alone is not normal. -/
   408	def Normal3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
   409	  EdgeLinkConnected τ ∧ VertexLinkConnected τ
   410	
   411	/-- `Clean3Complex τ`: pure, triangle-bounded, and normal — the honest
   412	"simplicial triangulation of a 3-ball" invariant the public shelling must carry,
   413	not the boundary-only trace.  Exposed under the `Is`-prefixed names
   414	`IsClean3Complex` / `IsNormal3Psman` as the preferred public vocabulary. -/
   415	def Clean3Complex (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
   416	  Pure3 τ ∧ TriangleBounded3 τ ∧ Normal3 τ
   417	
   418	/-- **`IsClean3Complex`** — the clean (normal) 3-complex condition: pure, every triangle in one or two
   419	tets, and *both* links connected (`VertexLinkConnected` and `EdgeLinkConnected`).  This is the local
